Throughout this section, denote πn\pi^{n} as the greedy policy induced by {Qhn}h=1H\{Q^{n}_{h}\}_{h
=1}^{H}, and note that λhn,whn,Qhn,Vhn\lambda_{h}^{n},w_{h}^{n},Q_{h}^{n},V_{h}^{n} are defined in
Algorithm 3. We further remark that the expectation (for example: Vhπ​(s)V_{h}^{\pi}(s)) is taken
with respect to the transition kernel of the target task, i.e., P(∗,T+1)P^{(*,T+1)}.

Proof Overview: The proof of Theorem 5.9 consists of two main steps and a final suboptimality gap
analysis. Step 1: We bound the difference between the estimated action value function rh​(s,a)+⟨ϕ^
h​(s,a),whn⟩r_{h}(s,a)+\langle\widehat{\phi}_{h}(s,a),w_{h}^{n}\rangle in Algorithm 3 and the true
action value function Qhπ​(s,a)Q_{h}^{\pi}(s,a) under a certain policy π\pi recursively as shown in
Lemma D.2. Step 2: We prove the estimated action value function QhnQ_{h}^{n} in Algorithm 3 is
near-optimistic with respect to the optimal true action value function over steps as shown in Lemma
 D.3. Our main technical contribution lies in capturing the impact of the misspecification of the
representation taken from upstream learning on these two steps. Suboptimality gap analysis: Based
on the first two steps, we first decompose the value function difference recursively, and then
obtain a final suboptimality gap.

D.1 Bounding the action value function difference

Following the proof similar to that for Lemma C.3, we introduce the concentration lemma for online
RL that upper-bounds the stochastic noise in regression.

D.2 Proving optimism of value function

Lemma D.3.

Under the setting of Theorem 5.9, with probability at least 1−δ/21-\delta/2, for any s∈𝒮,a∈𝒜,h∈
[H],n∈[No​n]s\in\mathcal{S},a\in\mathcal{A},h\in[H],n\in[N_{on}], we have

 Qhn​(s,a)≥Qh∗​(s,a)−2​(H−h+1)​ξdown.\displaystyle Q_{h}^{n}(s,a)\geq Q^{*}_{h}(s,a)-2(H-h+1)  (61)
 \xi_{\mathrm{down}}.

Proof.

We prove this lemma by induction. First, for step HH, by Lemma D.2, we have

  |(rH​(s,a)+⟨ϕ^H​(s,a),wHn⟩)−QHπ​(s,a)|\displaystyle\left|\left(r_{H}(s,a)+\langle\widehat{\phi}_
  {H}(s,a),w_{H}^{n}\rangle\right)-Q_{H}^{\pi}(s,a)\right|
  =|PH(∗,T+1)​(VH+1n−VH+1π)​(s,a)+ΔHk​(s,a)|\displaystyle\quad=\left|P_{H}^{(*,T+1)}(V_{H+1}^{n}
  -V_{H+1}^{\pi})(s,a)+\Delta_{H}^{k}(s,a)\right|
  ≤βn​‖ϕ^H​(s,a)‖(ΛHn)−1+2​ξdown.\displaystyle\quad\leq\beta_{n}\left\lVert\widehat{\phi}_{H}(s,a)
  \right\rVert_{(\Lambda_{H}^{n})^{-1}}+2\xi_{\mathrm{down}}.

Thus,

 QHn​(s,a)\       =min⁡{rH​(⋅,⋅)+⟨ϕ^​(⋅,⋅),wHn⟩+βn​‖ϕ^​(⋅,⋅)‖(ΛHn)−1,1}\displaystyle=\min\left\{r_

 {H}^{n}(s,a)     left\lVert\widehat{\phi}(\cdot,\cdot)\right\rVert_{(\Lambda_{H}^{n})^{-1}},1\
                  right\}
                  ≥QH∗​(s,a)−2​ξdown.\displaystyle\geq Q^{*}_{H}(s,a)-2\xi_{\mathrm{down}}.

Suppose Equation 61 holds for step h+1h+1. Then for step hh, following from Lemma D.2, we have:

 (rh​(s,a)CLOSE\      OPEN+⟨ϕ^h​(s,a),whn⟩)−Qh∗​(s,a)\displaystyle+\langle\widehat{\phi}_{h}

 {h}(s,a)
                      =Δhn​(s,a)+Ph(∗,T+1)​(Vh+1n−Vh+1∗)​(s,a)\displaystyle=\Delta_{h}^{n}(s,a)+P_
                      {h}^{(*,T+1)}(V_{h+1}^{n}-V_{h+1}^{*})(s,a)
                      ≥−βn​‖ϕ^h​(s,a)‖(Λhn)−1−2​ξdown−2​(H−h)​ξdown\displaystyle\geq-\beta_{n}\
                      left\lVert\widehat{\phi}_{h}(s,a)\right\rVert_{(\Lambda_{h}^{n})^{-1}}-2\xi_
                      {\mathrm{down}}-2(H-h)\xi_{\mathrm{down}}
                      ≥−βn​‖ϕ^h​(s,a)‖(Λhn)−1−2​(H−h+1)​ξdown.\displaystyle\geq-\beta_{n}\left\
                      lVert\widehat{\phi}_{h}(s,a)\right\rVert_{(\Lambda_{h}^{n})^{-1}}-2(H-h+1)\
                      xi_{\mathrm{down}}.

Therefore,

 Qhn​(s,a)\       =min⁡{rh​(⋅,⋅)+⟨ϕ^​(⋅,⋅),whn⟩+βn​‖ϕ^​(⋅,⋅)‖(Λhn)−1,1}\displaystyle=\min\left\{r_
 displaystyle Q_  {h}(\cdot,\cdot)+\langle\widehat{\phi}(\cdot,\cdot),w_{h}^{n}\rangle+\beta_{n}\
 {h}^{n}(s,a)     left\lVert\widehat{\phi}(\cdot,\cdot)\right\rVert_{(\Lambda_{h}^{n})^{-1}},1\
                  right\}
                  ≥Qh∗​(s,a)−2​(H−h+1)​ξdown,\displaystyle\geq Q^{*}_{h}(s,a)-2(H-h+1)\xi_{\mathrm
                  {down}},

which finishes the proof. ∎

D.3 Suboptimality gap: proof of Theorem 5.9

Before proving Theorem 5.9, we introduce the following lemma to decompose the value function
difference recursively.

Lemma D.4.

Fix δ∈(0,1)\delta\in(0,1). Let δhn=Vhn​(shn)−Vhπn​(shn)\delta_{h}^{n}=V_{h}^{n}(s_{h}^{n})-V_{h}^{\
pi^{n}}(s_{h}^{n}) and ξh+1n=𝔼[δh+1n|shn,ahn]−δh+1n\xi_{h+1}^{n}=\mathbb{E}\left[\delta_{h+1}^{n}|
s_{h}^{n},a_{h}^{n}\right]-\delta_{h+1}^{n}. Then, with probability at least 1−δ/21-\delta/2, for
h∈[H],n∈[No​n]h\in[H],n\in[N_{on}]:

 δhn≤δh+1n+ξh+1n+2​βn​‖ϕ^h​(shn,ahn)‖(Λhn)−1+2​ξdown.\displaystyle\delta_{h}^{n}\leq\delta_{h+1}^
 {n}+\xi_{h+1}^{n}+2\beta_{n}\left\lVert\widehat{\phi}_{h}(s_{h}^{n},a_{h}^{n})\right\rVert_{(\
 Lambda_{h}^{n})^{-1}}+2\xi_{\mathrm{down}}.

Proof.

By Lemma D.2, with probability at least 1−δ/21-\delta/2, for any s∈𝒮,a∈𝒜,h∈[H],n∈[No​n]s\in\mathcal
{S},a\in\mathcal{A},h\in[H],n\in[N_{on}], we have

  Qhn​(s,a)−Qhπn​(s,a)\displaystyle Q_{h}^{n}(s,a)-Q_{h}^{\pi^{n}}(s,a)
  =Δhn​(s,a)+Ph(∗,T+1)​(Vh+1n−Vh+1πn)​(s,a)\displaystyle\quad=\Delta_{h}^{n}(s,a)+P_{h}^{(*,T+1)}
  (V_{h+1}^{n}-V_{h+1}^{\pi^{n}})(s,a)
  ≤βn​‖ϕ^h​(s,a)‖(Λhn)−1+2​ξdown+Ph(∗,T+1)​(Vh+1n−Vh+1πn)​(s,a).\displaystyle\quad\leq\beta_{n}\
  left\lVert\widehat{\phi}_{h}(s,a)\right\rVert_{(\Lambda_{h}^{n})^{-1}}+2\xi_{\mathrm{down}}+P_
  {h}^{(*,T+1)}(V_{h+1}^{n}-V_{h+1}^{\pi^{n}})(s,a).

By the definition of πn\pi^{n} in Algorithm 3, we have πn​(shn)=ahn=arg⁡maxa∈𝒜​Qhn​(sh,a)\pi^{n}(s_
{h}^{n})=a_{h}^{n}={\arg\max}_{a\in\mathcal{A}}Q_{h}^{n}(s_{h},a). Then Qhn​(shn,ahn)−Qhπn​
(shn,ahn)=Vhn​(shn)−Vhπn​(shn)=δhnQ_{h}^{n}(s_{h}^{n},a_{h}^{n})-Q_{h}^{\pi^{n}}(s_{h}^{n},a_{h}^
{n})=V_{h}^{n}(s_{h}^{n})-V_{h}^{\pi^{n}}(s_{h}^{n})=\delta_{h}^{n}. Thus,

 δhn≤δh+1n+ξh+1n+2​βn​‖ϕ^h​(shn,ahn)‖(Λhn)−1+2​ξdown.\displaystyle\delta_{h}^{n}\leq\delta_{h+1}^
 {n}+\xi_{h+1}^{n}+2\beta_{n}\left\lVert\widehat{\phi}_{h}(s_{h}^{n},a_{h}^{n})\right\rVert_{(\
 Lambda_{h}^{n})^{-1}}+2\xi_{\mathrm{down}}.

∎

Finally, we combine Lemmas D.1, D.2, D.3 and D.4 to prove Theorem 5.9.

The regret can be bounded by

Furthermore, if the linear combination misspecification error ξ\xi (Assumption 5.4) is O~​(d/Non)\

then ξdown\xi_{\mathrm{down}} reduces to O~​(d/Non)\tilde{O}(\sqrt{d}/\sqrt{N_{\mathrm{on}}}) by

gap thus becomes

 O~(Hd1/2Non−1/2max{d,p}).\displaystyle\widetilde{O}\left(Hd^{1/2}N_{\mathrm{on}}^{-1/2}\max\{d,\
 sqrt{p}\}\right).

∎

Appendix E Auxiliary Lemmas

In this section, we provide several lemmas that are commonly used for the analysis of MDP problems.

The following lemma (Dann et al.,, 2017) will be useful to measure the difference between two value
functions under two MDPs and reward functions. We define PhVh+1(sh,ah)=𝔼s∼Ph(⋅|sh,ah)[V(s)]P_{h}V_
{h+1}(s_{h},a_{h})=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim P_{h}(\cdot|s_{h},a_{h})}\left[V(s)\right] as a shorthand
notation.

Lemma E.1.

(Simulation lemma). Suppose P1P_{1} and P2P_{2} are two MDPs and r1r_{1}, r2r_{2} are the
corresponding reward functions. Given a policy π\pi, we have,

 Vh,P1,r1π\
 displaystyle V_ (sh)−Vh,P2,r2π​(sh)\displaystyle(s_{h})-V_{h,P_{2},r_{2}}^{\pi}(s_{h})
 {h,P_{1},r_{1}}
 ^{\pi}
                 =∑h′=hH𝔼sh′∼(P2,π)ah′∼π[r1​(sh′,ah′)−r2​(sh′,ah′)+(P1,h′−P2,h′)​Vh′+1,P1,rπ​
                 (sh′,ah′)|sh]\displaystyle=\sum_{{h^{\prime}}=h}^{H}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{s_{h^{\
                 prime}}\sim(P_{2},\pi)\atop a_{h^{\prime}}\sim\pi}\left[r_{1}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h
                 ^{\prime}})-r_{2}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})+(P_{1,{h^{\prime}}}-P_{2,{h^{\
                 prime}}})V^{\pi}_{{h^{\prime}}+1,P_{1},r}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})|s_{h}\
                 right]
                 =∑h′=hH𝔼sh′∼(P1,π)ah′∼π[r1​(sh′,ah′)−r2​(sh′,ah′)+(P1,h′−P2,h′)​Vh′+1,P2,rπ​
                 (sh′,ah′)|sh].\displaystyle=\sum_{{h^{\prime}}=h}^{H}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{s_{h^{\
                 prime}}\sim(P_{1},\pi)\atop a_{h^{\prime}}\sim\pi}\left[r_{1}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h
                 ^{\prime}})-r_{2}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})+(P_{1,{h^{\prime}}}-P_{2,{h^{\
                 prime}}})V^{\pi}_{{h^{\prime}}+1,P_{2},r}(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})|s_{h}\
                 right].

The following lemma is essential in bounding the suboptimality in downstream offline RL (see
Lemma 3.1 in Jin et al., 2021b ()).

Lemma E.2.

Let {π^h}h=1H\{\widehat{\pi}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H} be the policy such that V^h(s)=⟨Q^h(s,⋅),π^h(⋅|s)⟩𝒜\

and ζh​(s,a)=(𝔹h​V^h+1​(s,a))−Q^h​(s,a)\zeta_{h}(s,a)=(\mathbb{B}_{h}\widehat{V}_{h+1}(s,a))-\

 V1π​(s)−V1π^​(s)\            =∑h=1H𝔼π​[ζh​(sh,aa)|s1=s]−∑h=1H𝔼π^​[ζh​(sh,ah)|s1=s]\displaystyle=\

                              +∑h=1H𝔼π[⟨Q^h(sh,⋅),πh(⋅|sh)−π^(⋅|sh)⟩|s1=s],\displaystyle\qquad+\
                              sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{\pi}[\langle\widehat{Q}_{h}(s_{h},\cdot),\
                              pi_{h}(\cdot|s_{h})-\widehat{\pi}(\cdot|s_{h})\rangle|s_{1}=s],

where the expectation is taken over sh,ahs_{h},a_{h}.

The following lemma is a standard inequality in the regret analysis for linear MDPs in
reinforcement learning (see Lemma G.2 in Agarwal et al., (2020) and Lemma 10 in Uehara et al.,
(2021)).

Lemma E.3.

(Elliptical potential lemma). Consider a sequence of d×dd\times d positive semidefinite matrices
X1,…,XNX_{1},\dots,X_{N} with tr⁡(Xn)≤1{\rm tr}(X_{n})\leq 1 for all n∈[N]n\in[N]. Define M0=λ0​IM_
{0}=\lambda_{0}I and Mn=Mn−1+XnM_{n}=M_{n-1}+X_{n}. Then

 ∑n=1Ntr⁡(Xn​Mn−1−1)≤2​log​det(MN)−2​log​det(M0)≤2​d​log⁡(1+Nd​λ0).\sum_{n=1}^{N}{\rm tr}\left(X_
 {n}M_{n-1}^{-1}\right)\leq 2\log\det(M_{N})-2\log\det(M_{0})\leq 2d\log\left(1+\frac{N}{d\lambda_
 {0}}\right).

Next, we introduce some useful inequalities that help convert the finite sample error bound into
the sample complexity.

Lemma E.4 (ε\varepsilon-Covering Number).

Let 𝒱\mathcal{V} denote a class of function mapping from 𝒮\mathcal{S} to ℝ\mathbb{R} with the
following parametric form

 V⁡(⋅)=min⁡{maxa∈𝒜⁡r⁡(⋅,a)+w⊤​ϕ​(⋅,a)+α​ϕ​(⋅,a)⊤​Λ−1​ϕ​(⋅,a),1},\displaystyle V(\cdot)=\min\left\
 {\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}r(\cdot,a)+w^{\top}\phi(\cdot,a)+\alpha\sqrt{\phi(\cdot,a)^{\top}\Lambda^
 {-1}\phi(\cdot,a)},1\right\},

where the parameters (r,w,β,Λ)(r,w,\beta,\Lambda) satisfy r∈ℛr\in\mathcal{R}, ‖w‖≤L\left\lVert w\
right\rVert\leq L, α∈[0,B]\alpha\in[0,B] and Σ⪰λ​I\Sigma\succeq\lambda I. Assume ‖ϕ⁡(s,a)‖≤1\left\
lVert\phi(s,a)\right\rVert\leq 1 for all (s,a)(s,a) pairs, and let 𝒩⁡(ε,r,R,B,λ)\mathcal{N}(\
varepsilon;r,R,B,\lambda) be the ε\varepsilon-covering number of 𝒱\mathcal{V} with respect to the
distance dist⁡(V,V′)=sups|V⁡(s)−V′​(s)|\mathrm{dist}(V,V^{\prime})=\sup_{s}|V(s)-V^{\prime}(s)|.
Further let 𝒩ℛ​(ε)\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{R}}(\varepsilon) be the ϵ\epsilon-covering number of
function class ℛ\mathcal{R}. Then

 log|𝒩⁡(ϵ,R,B,λ)|≤d​log⁡(1+6​R/ε)+d2​log⁡(1+18​d1/2​B2/(ε2​λ))+log⁡𝒩ℛ​(ε3)\displaystyle\log|\
 mathcal{N}(\epsilon;R,B,\lambda)|\leq d\log(1+6R/\varepsilon)+d^{2}\log(1+18d^{1/2}B^{2}/(\
 varepsilon^{2}\lambda))+\log\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{R}}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{3}\big)

where 𝒩ℛ​(ϵ3)\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{R}}(\frac{\epsilon}{3}) is the ϵ3\frac{\epsilon}{3} covering
number with respect to the reward function class ℛ\mathcal{R}.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as that in Jin et al., (2020) except that the function r⁡(s,a)r
(s,a) is not necessarily linear with respect to the representation ϕ⁡(s,a)\phi(s,a), and the
function ϕ\phi is selected from a function class Φ\Phi.

Reparametrize the function class 𝒱\mathcal{V} by letting A=α2​Λ−1A=\alpha^{2}\Lambda^{-1}, and we
have

 max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}r(\cdot,a)+w^{\top}\phi(\cdot,a)+\sqrt{\phi(\cdot,a)^{\top}A\phi(\cdot,a)},1
 \right\},

where r∈ℛr\in\mathcal{R}, ‖w‖≤R\left\lVert w\right\rVert\leq R, and ‖A‖≤B2​λ−1\left\lVert A\right\
rVert\leq B^{2}\lambda^{-1}. For any two functions V1,V2∈𝒱V_{1},V_{2}\in\mathcal{V}, let them take
the above form with parameters (r1,w1,A1)(r_{1},w_{1},A_{1}) and (r2,w2,A2)(r_{2},w_{2},A_{2}),
respectively. Since both min⁡{⋅,1}\min\{\cdot,1\} and maxa\max_{a} are contractions, we have

  dist⁡(V1,V2)\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}(V_{1},V_{2})
  ≤sups,a|[r1(s,a)+w1⊤ϕ(s,a)+ϕ​(s,a)⊤​A1​ϕ​(s,a)]\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,a}\left|\left[r_{1}
  (s,a)+w_{1}^{\top}\phi(s,a)+\sqrt{\phi(s,a)^{\top}A_{1}\phi(s,a)}\right]\right.
  −[r2(s,a)+w2⊤ϕ(s,a)+ϕ​(s,a)⊤​A2​ϕ​(s,a)]|\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\left.\left
  [r_{2}(s,a)+w_{2}^{\top}\phi(s,a)+\sqrt{\phi(s,a)^{\top}A_{2}\phi(s,a)}\right]\right|
  ≤sups,a|r1(s,a)−r2(s,a)|+supϕ:‖ϕ‖≤1|[w1⊤ϕ+ϕ⊤​A1​ϕ]−[w2⊤ϕ+ϕ⊤​A2​ϕ]|\displaystyle\leq\sup_
  {s,a}\left|r_{1}(s,a)-r_{2}(s,a)\right|+\sup_{\phi:\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert\leq 1}\left|

  ^{\top}A_{2}\phi}\right]\right|
  ≤sups,a|r1(s,a)−r2(s,a)|+supϕ:‖ϕ‖≤1|(w1−w2)⊤ϕ|+supϕ:‖ϕ‖≤1|ϕ⊤​(A1−A2)​ϕ|\displaystyle\leq\
  sup_{s,a}\left|r_{1}(s,a)-r_{2}(s,a)\right|+\sup_{\phi:\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert\leq 1}\
  left|(w_{1}-w_{2})^{\top}\phi\right|+\sup_{\phi:\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert\leq 1}\sqrt{\
  left|\phi^{\top}(A_{1}-A_{2})\phi\right|}
  =sups,a|r1​(s,a)−r2​(s,a)|+‖w1−w2‖+‖A1−A2‖\displaystyle=\sup_{s,a}\left|r_{1}(s,a)-r_{2}
  (s,a)\right|+\left\lVert w_{1}-w_{2}\right\rVert+\sqrt{\left\lVert A_{1}-A_{2}\right\rVert}
  ≤sups,a|r1​(s,a)−r2​(s,a)|+‖w1−w2‖+‖A1−A2‖F,\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,a}\left|r_{1}(s,a)-r_
  {2}(s,a)\right|+\left\lVert w_{1}-w_{2}\right\rVert+\sqrt{\left\lVert A_{1}-A_{2}\right\     (66)
  rVert_{\mathrm{F}}},

where the second to last inequality follows from the fact that |x−y|≤|x−y||\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\leq\
sqrt{|x-y|} for any x,y≥0x,y\geq 0. For matrices, ‖⋅‖\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert and ‖⋅‖F\left\
lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}} denote the matrix operator norm and Frobenius norm,
respectively.

Let 𝒞ℛ\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{R}} be an ε3\frac{\varepsilon}{3}-cover of ℛ\mathcal{R} such that |𝒞ℛ|=
𝒩ℛ​(ε3)|\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{R}}|=\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{R}}(\frac{\varepsilon}{3}). Let 𝒞w\mathcal
{C}_{w} be an ε3\frac{\varepsilon}{3}-cover of {w∈ℝd|‖w‖≤R}\{w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}|\left\lVert w\right
\rVert\leq R\} with respect to the l2l_{2}-norm of a vector, and let 𝒞A\mathcal{C}_{A} be an ε29\
frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{9}-cover of {A∈ℝd×d|‖A‖F≤d1/2​B2​λ−1}\{A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}|\left\lVert
A\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq d^{1/2}B^{2}\lambda^{-1}\} with respect to the Frobenius norm. By
Lemma D.5 in Jin et al., (2020), it holds that

 |𝒞w|≤(1+6​R/ε)d,|𝒞A|≤[1+18​d1/2​B2/(λ​ε2)]d2.\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{w}|\leq(1+6R/\varepsilon)
 ^{d},\qquad|\mathcal{C}_{A}|\leq[1+18d^{1/2}B^{2}/(\lambda\varepsilon^{2})]^{d^{2}}.

By Equation 66, for any V1∈VV_{1}\in V, there exists r2∈𝒞ℛr_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{R}},
w2∈𝒞ww_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{w} and AA such that V2V_{2} parametrized by (r2,w2,A2)(r_{2},w_{2},A_{2})
satisfies dist⁡(V1,V2)≤ε\mathrm{dist}(V_{1},V_{2})\leq\varepsilon. Hence, it holds that 𝒩⁡(ϵ,R,B,λ)
≤|𝒩ℛ​(ε3)|⋅|𝒞w|⋅|𝒞A|\mathcal{N}(\epsilon;R,B,\lambda)\leq|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{R}}(\frac{\
varepsilon}{3})|\cdot|\mathcal{C}_{w}|\cdot|\mathcal{C}_{A}|, which yields the desired result. ∎

Instructions for reporting errors

We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility
and mobile support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and
rendering, choose any of the methods listed below:

  • Click the "Report Issue" ( ) button, located in the page header.

Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.

Our team has already identified the following issues. We appreciate your time reviewing and
reporting rendering errors we may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML
versions for all readers, because disability should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank
you for your continued support in championing open access for all.

Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML
maintain a list of packages that need conversion, and welcome developer contributions.

We gratefully acknowledge support from our major funders, member institutions, , and all
contributors.
